### 20+ AI Public Perception Statistics

> 📊 **20 verified statistics** | Full page: [https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception)

Public attitudes toward AI are complex — a mix of excitement, concern, and pragmatic acceptance. Half of Americans are more concerned than excited, yet 83% globally believe AI will deliver benefits. These statistics, drawn from Pew Research, KPMG's 47-country study, and Exploding Topics original research, capture how different demographics and regions perceive AI.

#### Overall Sentiment

- <a id="perc-os1"></a>**50%** of US adults say AI in daily life makes them more concerned than excited
  - *Source: Pew Research (2025)* — [Original source](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2026/03/12/key-findings-about-how-americans-view-artificial-intelligence/) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-os1)
  - Up from 37% when Pew first asked in 2021. Just 10% say they are more excited than concerned; 38% are equally concerned and excited.
  - **Cite:** 50% — of US adults say AI in daily life makes them more concerned than excited. AI Statistics Center, citing Pew Research (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-os1

- <a id="perc-os2"></a>**16%** median share across 25 countries who are mainly excited about AI's rise
  - *Source: Pew Research Global (2025)* — [Original source](https://www.pewresearch.org/global/2025/10/15/how-people-around-the-world-view-ai/) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-os2)
  - In no country surveyed do more than 3 in 10 adults say they are mainly excited. A median of 34% are more concerned than excited; 42% are equally concerned and excited.
  - **Cite:** 16% — median share across 25 countries who are mainly excited about AI's rise. AI Statistics Center, citing Pew Research Global (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-os2

- <a id="perc-os3"></a>**83%** of people globally believe AI will result in a wide range of benefits
  - *Source: KPMG / University of Melbourne (2025)* — [Original source](https://kpmg.com/xx/en/our-insights/ai-and-technology/trust-attitudes-and-use-of-ai.html) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-os3)
  - The KPMG global study surveyed over 48,000 people across 47 countries — showing that despite concerns, a strong majority see upside.
  - **Cite:** 83% — of people globally believe AI will result in a wide range of benefits. AI Statistics Center, citing KPMG / University of Melbourne (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-os3

- <a id="perc-os4"></a>**74.46%** of internet users are at least a little worried about the environmental impact of AI
  - *Source: Exploding Topics (2025)* — [Original source](https://explodingtopics.com/blog/ai-trust-gap-research) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-os4)
  - More than a third (34.46%) say it worries them 'a lot'. A ChatGPT request uses 10× the electricity of a traditional Google search.
  - **Cite:** 74.46% — of internet users are at least a little worried about the environmental impact of AI. AI Statistics Center, citing Exploding Topics (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-os4

#### Trust & Skepticism

- <a id="perc-ts1"></a>**46%** of people globally are willing to trust AI systems
  - *Source: KPMG / University of Melbourne (2025)* — [Original source](https://kpmg.com/xx/en/our-insights/ai-and-technology/trust-attitudes-and-use-of-ai.html) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-ts1)
  - Less than half of the 48,000+ respondents across 47 countries trust AI — a critical barrier to further adoption.
  - **Cite:** 46% — of people globally are willing to trust AI systems. AI Statistics Center, citing KPMG / University of Melbourne (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-ts1

- <a id="perc-ts2"></a>**8.5%** of people say they always trust AI Overviews in search results
  - *Source: Exploding Topics (2025)* — [Original source](https://explodingtopics.com/blog/ai-trust-gap-research) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-ts2)
  - 61.17% only 'sometimes' trust them, and 21.05% never trust them — meaning ~82% are at least somewhat sceptical of AI-generated search.
  - **Cite:** 8.5% — of people say they always trust AI Overviews in search results. AI Statistics Center, citing Exploding Topics (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-ts2

- <a id="perc-ts3"></a>**71.15%** of search users have experienced at least one significant mistake in an AI Overview
  - *Source: Exploding Topics (2025)* — [Original source](https://explodingtopics.com/blog/ai-trust-gap-research) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-ts3)
  - The biggest issue: 42.1% encountered inaccurate or misleading content, 35.82% found missing context, and 16.78% received unsafe advice.
  - **Cite:** 71.15% — of search users have experienced at least one significant mistake in an AI Overview. AI Statistics Center, citing Exploding Topics (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-ts3

- <a id="perc-ts4"></a>**50.3%** of people would be less likely to engage with content marked as AI-generated
  - *Source: Exploding Topics (2025)* — [Original source](https://explodingtopics.com/blog/ai-trust-gap-research) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-ts4)
  - Only 18.51% would be more likely to engage. Women are more put off (55.57%) than men (42.54%).
  - **Cite:** 50.3% — of people would be less likely to engage with content marked as AI-generated. AI Statistics Center, citing Exploding Topics (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-ts4

#### Generational & Demographic Divides

- <a id="perc-gd1"></a>**49%** of Gen Z trust AI to be 'objective and accurate' — vs. just 18% of Boomers
  - *Source: Barna (2024)* — [Original source](https://www.barna.com/research/generations-ai/) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-gd1)
  - Based on a survey of 1,500 US adults. 45% of Boomers flat out say 'I don't trust it', compared with only 18% of Gen Z.
  - **Cite:** 49% — of Gen Z trust AI to be 'objective and accurate' — vs. just 18% of Boomers. AI Statistics Center, citing Barna (2024). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-gd1

- <a id="perc-gd2"></a>**~50%** of US adults under 50 interact with AI about once a day or more
  - *Source: Pew Research (2025)* — [Original source](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2026/03/12/key-findings-about-how-americans-view-artificial-intelligence/) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-gd2)
  - Smaller shares of those 50 and older say the same. 38% of employed 18–29-year-olds have used ChatGPT at work, vs. 18% of those 50+.
  - **Cite:** ~50% — of US adults under 50 interact with AI about once a day or more. AI Statistics Center, citing Pew Research (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-gd2

- <a id="perc-gd3"></a>**84.89%** of people aged 60+ want the same amount or less AI-generated content online
  - *Source: Exploding Topics (2025)* — [Original source](https://explodingtopics.com/blog/ai-trust-gap-research) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-gd3)
  - Older adults are most sceptical of AI content — yet 18–29-year-olds are the next-most-sceptical group, with just 16% wanting more.
  - **Cite:** 84.89% — of people aged 60+ want the same amount or less AI-generated content online. AI Statistics Center, citing Exploding Topics (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-gd3

- <a id="perc-gd4"></a>**55.57%** of women would be less likely to engage with AI-labelled content, vs. 42.54% of men
  - *Source: Exploding Topics (2025)* — [Original source](https://explodingtopics.com/blog/ai-trust-gap-research) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-gd4)
  - A gender gap runs throughout AI perception — in more than half of 25 countries surveyed by Pew, women are more likely than men to be mainly concerned about AI.
  - **Cite:** 55.57% — of women would be less likely to engage with AI-labelled content, vs. 42.54% of men. AI Statistics Center, citing Exploding Topics (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-gd4

#### Impact on Daily Life

- <a id="perc-il1"></a>**44%** of Americans say AI will have a positive impact on medical care over the next 20 years
  - *Source: Pew Research (2024)* — [Original source](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2026/03/12/key-findings-about-how-americans-view-artificial-intelligence/) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-il1)
  - Healthcare is the area where public sentiment is most positive — only 19% expect a negative impact. Views are far more pessimistic about education and jobs.
  - **Cite:** 44% — of Americans say AI will have a positive impact on medical care over the next 20 years. AI Statistics Center, citing Pew Research (2024). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-il1

- <a id="perc-il2"></a>**24%** say AI will have a positive impact on education — and just 23% say the same for jobs
  - *Source: Pew Research (2024)* — [Original source](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2026/03/12/key-findings-about-how-americans-view-artificial-intelligence/) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-il2)
  - Americans are roughly twice as optimistic about AI in healthcare as they are about AI in education or employment.
  - **Cite:** 24% — say AI will have a positive impact on education — and just 23% say the same for jobs. AI Statistics Center, citing Pew Research (2024). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-il2

- <a id="perc-il3"></a>**64%** of US teens ages 13–17 have used an AI chatbot; ~60% say classmates use them to cheat
  - *Source: Pew Research (2025)* — [Original source](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2026/03/12/key-findings-about-how-americans-view-artificial-intelligence/) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-il3)
  - One-in-ten teens say they use a chatbot for all or most schoolwork. About a third say cheating with chatbots happens 'extremely or very often' at their school.
  - **Cite:** 64% — of US teens ages 13–17 have used an AI chatbot; ~60% say classmates use them to cheat. AI Statistics Center, citing Pew Research (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-il3

- <a id="perc-il4"></a>**21.78%** of internet users want to see more AI-generated content online
  - *Source: Exploding Topics (2025)* — [Original source](https://explodingtopics.com/blog/ai-trust-gap-research) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-il4)
  - Meanwhile 48.12% want less, and a further 26% want about the same — meaning 74% would like a pause or reversal in AI content growth.
  - **Cite:** 21.78% — of internet users want to see more AI-generated content online. AI Statistics Center, citing Exploding Topics (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-il4

#### Regulation & The Expert-Public Gap

- <a id="perc-rg1"></a>**70%** of people globally believe national and international AI regulation is needed
  - *Source: KPMG / University of Melbourne (2025)* — [Original source](https://kpmg.com/xx/en/our-insights/ai-and-technology/trust-attitudes-and-use-of-ai.html) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-rg1)
  - A strong public mandate for AI governance across all 47 countries surveyed. 66% also admit relying on AI output without evaluating accuracy.
  - **Cite:** 70% — of people globally believe national and international AI regulation is needed. AI Statistics Center, citing KPMG / University of Melbourne (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-rg1

- <a id="perc-rg2"></a>**53%** median trust the EU to regulate AI effectively; 37% trust the US; 27% trust China
  - *Source: Pew Research Global (2025)* — [Original source](https://www.pewresearch.org/global/2025/10/15/how-people-around-the-world-view-ai/) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-rg2)
  - Across 25 countries, the EU is the most trusted AI regulator. Trust in own country varies from 89% in India to just 22% in Greece.
  - **Cite:** 53% — median trust the EU to regulate AI effectively; 37% trust the US; 27% trust China. AI Statistics Center, citing Pew Research Global (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-rg2

- <a id="perc-rg3"></a>**56%** of AI experts think AI will positively impact the US over 20 years — vs. just 17% of the public
  - *Source: Pew Research (2024)* — [Original source](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2026/03/12/key-findings-about-how-americans-view-artificial-intelligence/) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-rg3)
  - A wide expert-public gap. Yet half or more in both groups say they have little or no control over AI's use in their lives and want more.
  - **Cite:** 56% — of AI experts think AI will positively impact the US over 20 years — vs. just 17% of the public. AI Statistics Center, citing Pew Research (2024). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-rg3

- <a id="perc-rg4"></a>**44%** of Americans trust the US to regulate AI; 47% distrust — with a partisan split
  - *Source: Pew Research (2025)* — [Original source](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2026/03/12/key-findings-about-how-americans-view-artificial-intelligence/) · [Permalink](https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-rg4)
  - 54% of Republicans trust the US to regulate AI effectively, vs. only 36% of Democrats — reflecting broader partisan divides on tech governance.
  - **Cite:** 44% — of Americans trust the US to regulate AI; 47% distrust — with a partisan split. AI Statistics Center, citing Pew Research (2025). https://AIStatisticsCenter.com/statistics/ai-public-perception#perc-rg4
